- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1b. (1) A paste-folder part allows only letters, digits, `-`, `_` and `.` (no spaces, no `%`, `#`, `?`, `(`, `)`), stricter than the plan's "relative, no `..`, no drive": a space or a bracket in the folder would make the pasted link not plain Markdown, and a page validator and Rust that disagree is the failure ISC-368 guards against, so both read the 46 cases in `tests/data/paste-folders.json`. (2) A draft keeps its fixed `assets` folder (nothing is written next to a file that does not exist); `images.pasteFolder` applies at Save As time, which copies the used images into it and rewrites the draft's links to it, also for an image whose name did not change. ISC-330 reads "the next paste" for a file with a folder and "the next Save As" for a draft. (3) The page pushes validated runtime values to Rust with one command, `settings_apply`, which checks them again and refuses the whole batch if one is wrong (a value that guards a boundary is not taken on the page's word). (4) Developer tools: `tauri` gets the `devtools` feature, so `open_devtools` exists in a release build; the window config turns the native F12 off (`"devtools": false`) and the command `devtools_open` opens them only while the setting is on. WebView2 cannot close an open developer-tools window from the API on Windows, so turning the setting off stops new opens and leaves an open window open. ISC-249 stays open: its release-build half needs the owner's permission to build one. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1c. The Settings tab is a third tab kind, `settings`, whose record holds the group it shows; there is one because `openSettingsTab` looks for an existing one first (in any pane) and splitting never copies it, not because of a fixed id. A saved session that names two keeps the first in the layout's order (new ISC-388), and a tab of a kind this version does not know is left out instead of being opened as a file by its path (new ISC-387, which also covers a session written by a later version). A saved group name this version does not know falls back to the first group with no notice. The group is not in the layout, so a change to it bumps `tabRecordsVersion`, which the session journal watches; without that a group chosen by a click would reach `session.json` only with the next layout change. The gear is in the title bar, between the file name and the window buttons (the plan says only "a gear button" and names no place; say so if it belongs elsewhere). Until P8-T1d the screen shows the five groups and "This group has no settings yet." under each. The plan says this task's view loads lazily "like the palette and the search panel"; no claim covered the architecture rule for that yet, so the `settings-lazy` rule and its test are new ISC-389. Open settings.json opens the file as an ordinary text tab; the prompt for unsaved edits in it is P8-T1d's. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1d. Every row is one `SettingRow` (label, description, control slot, key, dot and reset while modified, error and lock notes), drawn from the table in `features/settings/rows.ts`, which holds only wording and grouping: values, defaults and checking stay in `SETTING_DEFS`. A control never holds a value. A switch or choice asks the store for the change at once; a number or text field keeps what is typed as a draft and checks it with the same `check` when it is left or Enter is pressed, and a refused value shows its reason on the row and writes nothing. The Appearance group has only Theme (one choice, Dark, until the themes of P8-T3), the fonts are P8-T1e's rows, and Keyboard has none until P8-T2, so it says so. ISC-358 stays open: only Dark exists to look at, and the plan moves it to P8-T3 with the other themes (tasks.md moves it in the P8-T1f commit). The unsaved-edits question (ISC-354) lives in the settings store, so a change is held in `pendingSettingChange` and the screen only shows the question: Save writes the tab and then makes the change, Don't save reloads the tab from the file (new `revertDocument`, which `resolvePendingRevert` now uses too) and makes it, Cancel drops it; a failed save keeps the question open. A change that is written now also brings an open, clean `settings.json` tab up to date through `refreshFromDisk`, without waiting for the file watcher (new ISC-391; the browser mock's document watcher never reports the page's own settings writes, and the real watcher was not relied on for this). The generated Switch styled itself with `data-checked:` and `data-unchecked:`, which Reka does not set, so it was invisible; tokens.css now defines those two variants (new ISC-390). `checkFolderName` accepts `..` as an excluded-folder name; it can never match a directory entry, so it is harmless and left as it is. The Links row, when `MDMATE_LINKS_ON_RENAME` is set, shows the variable's value, is disabled and drops its reset icon. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1e. The document fonts reach the editor as CSS custom properties on the page's root (`--doc-font-body`, `--doc-font-heading`, `--doc-font-code`, `--doc-size`, `--doc-code-ratio`, `--doc-heading-1` to `-6`), set by `editor/documentFonts.ts` from the live settings and read only by the editor's theme, so nothing outside the document can pick them up (new ISC-392). A family is written as the owner likes and is turned into a list of quoted names ending in the built-in stack, because an unquoted name that starts with a digit is invalid CSS and would drop the whole declaration instead of falling back. Headings fall back to the body family. The new look at the defaults is not the old look: the editor drew 13 px text (it inherited the window's), and the plan's default `fonts.size` is 15 with code at 14 (it was 0.92 of 13, about 12 px); the heading multiples are unchanged, so headings grow with the body. The code size is held as a multiple of the text size (`codeSize / size`) and code is drawn at `calc(ratio * 1em)`: code in body text and in a block is exactly `fonts.codeSize`, and inline code in a heading grows with the heading, as it did when it was `0.92em` (a first version held px and drew code in an H1 smaller than the heading). Code in a table sits in text drawn at 0.95em, so it is 0.95 x `fonts.codeSize`. Each family field also shows a sample line in the family typed, before it is accepted (new ISC-393).
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1f. A `Command` gains an optional `state()` (true is On) that the palette rows and the menu items show as On or Off, read from the live setting like `enabled`. Toggle Word Wrap, Toggle Developer Tools and Switch Theme are commands; the two toggles go through `requestSettingChange`, the same path as the Settings screen, so unsaved edits in the settings.json tab hold the change and the Settings tab is shown, where the question is (new ISC-394), and nothing is written until it is answered. Switch Theme is a third palette mode, `themes`, a pick list of `THEME_CHOICES` (moved to `lib/themes.ts` so the palette does not pull in the screen's row table) with the theme in use marked. Only Dark exists, so what is proven now is the list, the mark and the write path (new ISC-395); ISC-371 (Dark, Light, Sepia, System and every user theme file) and ISC-358 (the screen in three themes) need the themes and move to P8-T3. ISC-249 stays open: the command half is done, the release-build half waits for the owner's permission to build one.
- 2026-11-14: refined: a tooltip has its own surface, `--tooltip` and `--tooltip-foreground` (owner: it must stand out from the app background). Dark is lighter than the page; Light stays the popover color, since a darker tooltip did not suit it (owner, same day). The shadcn `TooltipContent` and the in-editor `.editor-hint` use them, so the theme has 56 tokens, not 54: ISC-381 and the P8-T3 plan now say 56.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T2 design. (1) `keybindings.json` is one flat object from command id to shortcut text, or to `null` for no shortcut; ids contain dots (`view.toggleSidebar`) and stay one key, never a path. A row's reset removes its key; Reset all writes `{}` (the plan says "clears the file"), which also drops keys for ids this build does not know, while a change of one row keeps them like ISC-349. (2) Overrides are resolved against the final set of bindings, not pairwise against the defaults, and the key order in the file means nothing: a swap works; when two commands' resulting shortcuts are the same, every override involved is dropped (the command goes back to its default) and the notice names the commands and the shortcut, repeated until nothing collides, since a default coming back can collide in turn. A default never loses to an override. Registering two built-in defaults on one shortcut still throws: that is a programmer error, bad user data never throws. (3) The ISC-374 row named `Mod+B` and Toggle Sidebar, but `Mod+B` is Bold and Toggle Sidebar is `Mod+Shift+B`; the row now records `Mod+Shift+B` on Bold. (4) New ISC-402 to ISC-411 for what the plan left implicit: a rebind beats CodeMirror's own keys in an open editor, `null`, the dirty `keybindings.json` tab, the recorder's edges, `bindingFromEvent`, Reset all, Open keybindings.json (the plan put it in Advanced, T1d did not add it), the banner, and a live label everywhere. (5) Rust gets a second file on the same code (`KeybindingsStore`, fixed name, no path from the page), so ISC-384 covers it. (6) ISC-411 first named the tab strip too; it was narrowed to the Keyboard row, the palette and the menu, the ones the test reads, since the tab strip and the tree menu call the same reactive function but were not mounted.
- 2026-11-14: refined: Keyboard page groups and search (owner-requested, decided in a grill with all answers agreed). (1) Six groups in a fixed order, each command naming exactly one: File 10, Find 5, Formatting 7, View 7, Tabs & Navigation 6, App 6; the `group` field is required on `Command` and `registerCommand` refuses an unlisted one, so a new command cannot be left off the page. Group names show only on the Keyboard page, not in the palette. (2) One scrolling page with a heading per group, rows A to Z inside, no collapsing; a group with no match has no heading. (3) The search box sits beside the "Keyboard" heading (not inside the `<h1>`, so the heading and the section label stay "Keyboard"); it uses the Search settings rule (every word must be part of the row) over title, id, group name and the shortcut as shown now and by default, with the refinement that a word holding a plus must equal whole plus-separated parts of one shortcut. (4) Search settings still lists no shortcut. (5) Starting to use the box cancels a recording through the shortcut button's blur (the capture listener keeps every key from the box while a row records), and the box starts empty each time a category is chosen. New ISC-412 to ISC-419.
- 2026-11-14: refined: settings.json and keybindings.json are JSON with comments (owner chose it after rejecting a read-only defaults tab; the full reasons are in `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`). Both read comments and trailing commas; opening either while it holds nothing writes a commented template of every setting or command; an edit changes only its key and never a comment; an entry taken out gets its commented example back, so all-defaults reads like the first template; Reset all does the same. Deviation from "template on open only": the first change from the screen to a file that holds nothing also starts from the template (ISC-429). The docs that said "strict JSON" (`architecture.md` and the phase `plan.md`) were reworded. New ISC-420 to ISC-430.
- 2026-11-14: refined: the restore (decision above) only runs in a file that carries the template, found by its first header comment. Owner screenshot: a `settings.json` that held an entry from before the template existed, after that entry was reset, held a lone commented `fonts` block that looked like a second `fonts` object. A file written by hand, or with its header deleted, now just loses the entry and gains no comment; a file left holding nothing takes the whole template on the next change (ISC-429). In a full template, a live group still sits above the commented block of the same group, which lists the other keys. New ISC-431.
- 2026-11-14: owner request, three UI changes. (1) Keyboard group headings: 16px semibold in the text color (were 12px, dim, upper case), 40px above each group after the first (were 20px). (2) Reset all moved from the Keyboard page to Advanced, one per file, each in the row of its file and behind a confirmation dialog (Cancel focused; Escape and Cancel change nothing). The visible label is "Reset all" and the accessible name is "Reset all settings" or "Reset all shortcuts": the full label made the two buttons wrap into a stack in the 16rem control column, and the owner asked for them beside each other. The dialog and the aria-label name the file. A reset asked for while the file's tab holds unsaved edits shows the existing save-first question, now one shared bar (SaveFirstQuestion) on the Settings screen for both files, because the keybindings question used to live only on the Keyboard page. (3) A --warning token, since none existed: Dark oklch(0.82 0.14 82), Light oklch(0.55 0.12 68). A first Light value (0.5 0.09 72) passed the tests but looked brown and close to the grey text in the window, so it was lightened and made more saturated; both pass the 4.5 contrast and sRGB tests. New ISC-432 to ISC-438.
- 2026-11-15: owner request, the two settings `editor.limitContentWidth` and `editor.contentMaxWidth` took effect nowhere. They now limit the text column of editor tabs. The limit is one custom property on the page's root (`--doc-content-max`, `calc(width + 2 * 1.5rem)` because the setting counts the text and not the padding, or `none`), read only by the editor theme as `max-width` with `margin-inline: auto`; the theme's own fallback is the default, so the first paint is already right. A change re-sets the property and asks each open view to measure (`documentWidth.ts`). Reading of "not the Settings / Keybindings tabs": the Settings screen is a Vue view with no editor, so it is exempt by construction; the `settings.json` and `keybindings.json` editor tabs keep the whole width, found by file name (any folder, any case, and following a rename). Flip: drop `fullWidthFor` in `document.ts` to limit them too. While word wrap is off the limit steps aside (`.cm-content:not(.cm-lineWrapping)`): CodeMirror gives a non-wrapping content box the width of its longest line, which beats `max-width`, so a limit there would be drawn wrong or cut a line. The definitions of the two settings (`settings.ts`, `settingsFile.ts`, `rows.ts`) were uncommitted work from another session; this commit carries them. ISC-439 to ISC-443.

- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T3 design. (1) The palette is the root's `data-theme` (`dark`, `light`, `sepia`) and darkness is the root's `dark` class, set only when the palette's `color-scheme` is dark, so Tailwind's `dark:` variants follow darkness and not a name: Sepia and any user theme based on a light palette never switch them on (`@custom-variant dark` stays as it was; `index.html` starts with `class="dark"` and `data-theme="dark"`, and the Tauri window keeps Dark's background, pinned by `tests/styles/themes.test.ts`). (2) There are 57 tokens, counted from the CSS (the plan said 56); the editor's groups are Core 21, Sidebar 5, Chrome 13, Editor 4 and Syntax 14, and `lib/themeTokens.ts` holds the list and the 31 contrast pairs once for the test and the editor. (3) A user theme is `themes/<name>.json`, JSON with comments: `{ "name"?, "base": dark|light|sepia, "colors": { token: value } }`, token names without the dashes. A value passes only if it is `#rgb`, `#rgba`, `#rrggbb`, `#rrggbbaa` or `oklch()` with plain numbers; not `var()`, `url()`, `rgb()` or a named color, so what reaches the styles has the shape the contrast code reads. A bad value is dropped (the token keeps its base color) with a notice; a file that does not parse, or a selected name with no file, shows Dark with a notice and leaves `settings.json` as written; a file named like a built-in (any case, and `system`) is ignored with one notice. Applying a theme goes through one function that checks each value again before `style.setProperty`. (4) The built-in values are read from the CSS files with `?raw` and the same declaration parser, so the CSS stays the one source and Customize copies what the browser has; vitest returns an empty string for `?raw` CSS unless `css.include` names the theme files (found when the Sepia tests saw 0 tokens), so `vite.config.ts` names them. (5) Rust: `ThemeStore` (list, read, write with a `create` flag, watch, ensure folder), no path from the page, a plain name plus the fixed `.json`, 256 KB per file, 200 files, only plain files (a link or folder is skipped and refused), a create never replaces. The name rules are tested against one data file, `tests/data/theme-names.json`, by both sides. The settings watcher loop became generic (`run` takes the event filter) instead of being copied. (6) Customize writes literal colors for every token of what is shown (System copies the Dark or Light the OS has made current; `var()` aliases are resolved), named `<Theme> copy`, then `copy 2` and so on, and takes the next name if a file appeared in the meantime; it then selects the copy through the settings screen's own path, so the unsaved-edits question still applies. (7) The colour editor shows every token of the theme in force, in groups, only for a user theme; a built-in shows a Customize button. A text field per token checks what is typed (Enter or leaving the field accepts, Escape drops); the picker exists only for a color with no alpha, because `<input type=color>` would drop it. A change is shown on the page at once and written 150 ms after the last one, read fresh from the file first and edited with `jsonc-parser` so comments and unknown keys stay; writes run one after another; a file that is not valid JSON is refused and left alone. A write that lands while a newer color is still pending re-shows the pending one (a test found the older file color flashing back). The contrast warnings are advisory and use the base's color for a token the file leaves out. (8) Open themes folder is a button on Advanced only. The plan and ISC-457 also named a palette command, but the owner agreed the exact 41 commands and their groups (ISC-412, `tests/workbench/defaultCommands.test.ts`), so the command was not added and ISC-457 now says so. (9) Sepia ports MarkdownWeave's cream page `#fdf6e3`, brown text `#3b2f1e`, links `#8b5e3c`; its grey notes `#8a7060` (4.26:1 on the page) and `#b8a090` (2.30:1) fall under the floors, so the greys are darker, and the syntax colors, `--warning` and the rest are designed for the page. Its own test then found three literal values copied from Light (`--find-match`, `--find-match-active`, `--syntax-number`); each now has its own. ISC-146 (no SublimeText assets) stays open for the owner: Dark and Light are the app's own and Sepia comes from the owner's MarkdownWeave. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.

- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T4 design. (1) The data folder is chosen in this order: `MDMATE_DATA_DIR` when set and not empty, then `<exe dir>/data` when a file named `mdmate.portable` is beside the exe, then the platform's app-data folder, then a temp folder. The environment variable wins over the marker because it is the one explicit, per-launch choice: tests and the perf harness set it to a fresh folder so they never touch a real session, and a copy of the app carrying a marker must not defeat that. (2) The marker has to be a file (`fs::metadata(..).is_file()`): a folder called `mdmate.portable` is not one, and only the exe's own folder is looked at, never the working folder or a parent. (3) Riftle's `paths.rs` was the model for the marker idea but not for its failures: it `expect`s on a missing exe path and `create_dir_all`s with a panic. Here an unreadable exe path or folder means "not portable" and the usual folder is used (ISC-461); no folder is created by the lookup, each store makes its own on first write, as before. (4) `data_dir` takes the exe folder as a parameter, so the tests inject a scratch folder and never depend on where the test binary sits. (5) The WebView2 profile stays where Tauri puts it (LocalAppData), so portable mode moves the app's own data and not the browser engine's cache. This is my reading of "moves all data there" and the owner confirmed on 2026-11-15 to leave it as is: the profile holds engine caches, not user data, and moving it needs a startup environment setting before the window exists; the README (P10) still has to say so. (6) `cfg(windows)` is not needed: the lookup uses only `std::env::current_exe` and `std::fs`, so the arch rule on OS code outside `platform/` is untouched. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.

- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T5 design (owner request after trying P8-T3). (1) Every sub-group heading on the Settings screen is one component, `SubGroupHeading.vue`, drawn in `--primary`: Theme, Fonts and Theme colors on Appearance, the five color groups, and the Keyboard groups. This reverses the 2026-11-14 request for headings in the text color; the owner asked for it, and `primary on background` is already a contrast pair at 4.5. The page title stays in the text color. A row names its sub-group in `SETTING_ROWS` (`group`), and the screen draws a heading where the group changes, none in a search. (2) `themes.hideContrastWarnings` is true by default: the owner first said "off by default", then, shown the switch's name, "Hide contrast warnings" on by default, so no contrast line shows until it is turned off. A color under its floor is written either way. (3) `themes.colorFormat` is `hex` or `oklch` and changes only how colors are shown and written. A field shows a color as written when it is already in the chosen format and converts it otherwise; typing either format is accepted and is written in the chosen one; the picker writes the chosen one. A value is converted only when it is edited, so a file is never rewritten by changing the format (ISC-470). (4) The OKLab conversion is Ottosson's, in `lib/color.ts`, printed with 5 decimals for lightness and chroma and 3 for hue so that hex to oklch to hex is exact on a grid of 4,913 colors; with 4 decimals one color (#00d0d0) came back one step off. The function name is kept in a constant because the arch scan forbids a color literal outside `src/styles`. (5) Found by the first test of the setting: `assignSettings` copies each settings group by hand, so a new group has to be added there or it never loads from `settings.json`.
- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T6 design (owner request, grill rounds 1 and 2). (1) A theme has a free display name (the `name` in its file, 1 to 60 printable characters, unique among themes ignoring case, never a built-in name); the file name is derived from it (`themeStem`: characters a file cannot have become `-`, a taken or built-in file name gets a number on the file only) and is the theme's identity, so Duplicate of "Mine" is now "Mine copy" (it was "mine copy"). (2) One list of every theme under a "Themes" heading (built-ins first, then the user's by name); New theme is an inline form, not a modal, because the project has no Dialog component, only alert-dialog, which is kept for the delete question. Customize is now "duplicate the theme in use". (3) "Based on" is a Dark, Light, Sepia button group, not a two-way switch, since a theme can sit on Sepia; changing it first writes the colors the file leaves out from the old base, so nothing on screen changes. (4) Delete goes to the Recycle Bin through the `trash` crate; with no bin it is refused and the file stays (never a permanent delete); a theme in use gives way to Dark with a notice. Rename and delete hold the "theme not found" notice (`holdThemeNotices`) while the file system catches up, otherwise renaming the theme in use would toast an error. (5) A reset button shows only on a color that differs from the base palette, compared as colors, so a value in another format does not count as changed. (6) The editor is scrolled into view by a request counter that SettingsView watches; it waits one turn after the next tick because a New theme form that closes right after the request moves everything below it (found in the real window: the heading landed 206 px above the top; ISC-481 now checks the form is closed at scroll time). (7) The Hide contrast warnings row is not in search (`inEditor`), as the owner asked it be seen only while editing. (8) Built-in themes have a real guard, `isBuiltInTheme` in `customize.ts`: rename is refused with a reason, delete returns false and Edit does nothing; before it, a built-in id was only refused by accident (Rust said `not-found` and a toast followed). (9) Deviations from the plan, recorded: no per-row "Show in folder" (the host has only "open the themes folder", which the Appearance page already offers as the "Open themes folder" button at its end, so a row action would do the same thing); no dot beside the per-color reset icon (the icon itself marks a changed color).
- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T6 follow-up (owner, after seeing the list). (1) A theme row ends with one button of one width (5rem): Select, or for the theme in use a disabled "In use" in the theme's own `success` color (text, outline and a 10% background); everything else (Edit colors, Rename, Duplicate, Delete) is in an ellipsis menu after it whose trigger has no border and no background, even under the pointer. A built-in's menu holds Duplicate only. (2) `success` is a new theme token (the 58th, in Core, next to `warning`, with a 4.5 contrast pair against the background): the arch rule forbids Tailwind palette colors outside src/styles, and a theme token is how the project adds a color; a theme file that omits it takes it from its base, so no existing theme changes. (3) Each swatch has a tooltip naming its token and what it is for (Background, Card, Foreground, Primary, Accent). (4) Duplicate (row menu) and Customize or Duplicate above the colors open one form, the one New theme uses, with the name "<name> copy" selected and no palette choice, and the screen scrolls it to the middle; Customize no longer writes anything itself. `copyTheme(of, name)` checks the name like New theme does. (5) Found in the real window: an open menu traps the focus, so a field that asks for focus at once is refused and the menu then gives the focus back to its button. The menu's close-auto-focus is prevented only for Rename and Duplicate, and their fields take the focus one turn after the menu has closed; any other item leaves the focus on the ellipsis button, where a keyboard user was. (6a) The outline button brings its own dark-scheme grey border and background (dark:bg-input, dark:border-input) that a plain bg-success/10 does not replace, so the In use button sets both dark: classes too, and a test checks the merged class list for the grey. (6) On the Advanced page the three file rows end in a right-aligned flex line and the three buttons are 10.25rem wide, so their right edges meet.
- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T6 second follow-up (owner, from screenshots). (1) "Based on" and "Start from" are one shadcn `ToggleGroup` each (added with the CLI, `@ts-nocheck` on the generated files like the others), wrapped in `ChoiceGroup.vue`: the chosen button has the primary background and text, pressing it again keeps it (reka reports "nothing chosen" then; the wrapper ignores it, so there is always an answer). The hover classes of the on state are set too, or the hover grey replaced the primary. (2) The form says "Name" with its field on the same line, for New theme and for a copy; the line about what a copy is stays under it. (3) A color with alpha has a picker: `<input type="color">` holds only `#rrggbb`, so the opacity is a separate percent field beside the swatch, and each keeps what the other set (`withOpacity` in `color.ts`). The field shows for a token that some built-in theme makes translucent (scrollbar thumb, its hover, drop preview, and find match and its active one, which the owner had not listed), or whose value has alpha now, so an opaque value can get its transparency back. A true alpha picker would need a custom widget; two controls that already exist were enough. (4) REVERSES the T6 decision "copies are verbatim": New theme, Duplicate and a base switch now write every color in the format chosen in Settings, converted when the source was in the other, because a file that mixes oklch and hex under the hex setting looked like a bug to the owner. The cost is rounding: a hex color from oklch is within one 8-bit step, and an oklch color outside sRGB is clamped.
- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T6 third follow-up (owner: "build the real alpha picker"). (1) Every color's swatch is the trigger of one popover (`ColorPicker.vue`: shadcn Popover, added with `pnpm ui:add`); the native `<input type="color">` and the row's percent field are gone. The percent field had been there for five tokens only, and the owner found that confusing: the only way to get it elsewhere was to type two more characters into the hex. (2) The area is reka-ui's `ColorArea` (pointer, keyboard, thumb); the hue and alpha sliders are reka's `Slider` with gradient tracks, because reka's own `ColorSlider` loses the hue at grey and black (read from its source and confirmed by calling it: the hue of a grey is 0 and setting a hue on a black or grey returns hue 0). The picker holds hue, saturation, brightness and alpha itself, so a hue picked while the color is grey or black is still there when it is not; `toHsv` and `fromHsv` in `color.ts` convert with no 8-bit step lost. (3) What the picker sent is remembered (as `#rrggbbaa`), and when it comes back as the value it is not read again: reading a rounded 8-bit color back would move the hue under the pointer. A typed value, Reset or a base switch comes back as a different color and is read. (4) Open and close sends nothing: HSV is sRGB, so re-sending an oklch or out-of-gamut value would convert it. (5) The gradients and the checkerboard live in `src/styles/colorpicker.css` (the arch rule keeps color literals there), imported by the component so it ships with the lazy Settings chunk; the swatch shows a checkerboard behind a translucent color. (6) A slip of mine in the last round, fixed here: I had added toggle-group with `shadcn-vue@latest` directly. The project has `pnpm ui:add` (pinned to 2.9.0, restores protected files, marks the generated files); the toggle files were regenerated through it and are identical in content. (7) Not done: `contrast()` still treats every color as opaque, so alpha on a text or surface color can make a pair look fine when it is not.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 fourth follow-up (owner: "are all the tokens used? I changed --selection and --caret and see nothing"). A scan of the 58 tokens found four nothing read: `--selection`, `--caret`, `--handle` and `--card-foreground`. (1) The editor never drew its own selection or caret, so the browser did, in its own colors, and `create.ts` set the native caret to `--foreground`. CodeMirror's `drawSelection()` is now on in both bases (`common` and `followerBase`: a tab of a document is a follower), and the `frame` theme colors the drawn parts: `.cm-cursor` and `.cm-dropCursor` get `border-left-color: var(--caret)`; the selection is `var(--selection)` for the focused rule (as specific as CodeMirror's own), the plain one, and `.cm-content ::selection` for text inside a widget. The native `caretColor` line is gone: drawSelection hides the native caret with `!important`, so it would have been a use that does nothing, the same bug again. A side effect that is wanted: `&.cm-image-selected .cm-cursorLayer { visibility: hidden }`, which was written for this and had nothing to hide, now works. (2) `--handle` is the split handle at rest (`bg-handle` from `SplitNodeView` over the generated `ResizableHandle` by tailwind-merge; the generated file is untouched) and, at the owner's request, the image resize handle at rest, with `--handle-active` under the pointer and while dragged (`:hover`, `:active`). (3) `--card-foreground` is the text color of the New theme form, which already used `--card` for its background; its label no longer sets its own color. (4) `tests/styles/tokenUse.test.ts` fails when a token has no use (comments, the `@theme` rename lines, the theme files and the perf lab do not count). It cannot see a use that is overridden, as the caret one was, so `tests/editor/tokenColors.test.ts` checks the injected rules, and the split handle's classes have their own test because the image handle also reads `--handle` and would hide them from the scan. (5) The sidebar edge is also a resize handle, and still uses `--ring` at 40 percent; not asked for, left alone. The text drop caret (`.cm-drop-caret`) still uses `--primary`. (6) The built-in `--handle` values are the quiet splitter line colors, so the image handle at rest is nearly invisible in Dark, Light and Sepia (read from screenshots); changing the built-in values or adding an outline is the owner's call.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 fifth follow-up (owner: "--handle and --handle-active do not affect the sidebar handle, only the pane handle"). I had left the sidebar edge on `--ring` because it was not asked for; it is a resize handle like the others, so it now uses the same tokens: `bg-handle` at rest, `bg-handle-active` hovered, focused (`focus-visible`) or pressed. It is 4 px wide (the pane handle is a 1 px line with a wider hit area), so the resting color is a 4 px band, which is subtle with the built-in `--handle` and clear with a bright one. It stays in the active color after a drag while it has focus, as the old `focus-visible:bg-ring/60` did: the edge takes focus when pressed so the arrow keys resize it (ISC-241).
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 sixth follow-up (owner: selected inline code, with its background, "looks unselected"). The inline code span paints its own background above the selection, which CodeMirror draws behind the text, so a selection over code was hidden. CSS cannot tell a span is selected, so `scan.ts` cuts the span at the selection: the part a non-empty selection covers is marked `cm-md-code cm-md-code-selected` (font as code, no background, `--foreground` text) and the rest keeps `cm-md-code`. The cut is exact, so a selection that ends inside a span leaves the rest of it as code. A caret, or a selection that only touches the span's edge, changes nothing. The app has one selection range (ISC-96), so the joining of several ranges in `selectedParts` is a pure function with its own test and not a reachable case. Not covered: code inside a table cell, which is a widget with its own styles (`.cm-md-table code`); the selection there is the native one and the cell's code background still hides it.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 seventh follow-up (owner: "--handle-active applies to both the active and non-active state of the resize handle, and to the image border; the resize handle is the small square and the border round the image"). This replaces the image half of the fourth follow-up, where the square was `--handle` at rest and `--handle-active` on hover. A selected image is one state, so its outline (`.cm-md-image-selected > .cm-md-image-frame`, which was `--primary`) and its resize square are both `--handle-active` and nothing else: no rest color, no hover or pressed rule. `--handle` stays the color of the split handle and the sidebar edge at rest. This also fixes the visibility the fourth follow-up reported: the square at rest was nearly invisible in the built-in themes, and is now as visible as the outline. ISC-502 now covers the split handle only; the image part is ISC-509.

- ISC-71: `node scripts/check-arch.mjs languages-lazy --dist <build>` on a build made with `vite build --sourcemap`: the chunks the page loads first (the entry script, the modulepreload link and what they import statically, 2 files, 790 kB) hold `lang-css`, `lang-html`, `lang-javascript` and their grammars and no other language package, while the language chunks and the list of them load on demand. The claim as written, "no parser in the entry bundle", is not literally true and cannot be: `@codemirror/lang-markdown` imports `lang-html` (which brings `lang-css` and `lang-javascript`) for the HTML it finds in Markdown. The rule allows those six packages by name and nothing else; the allowance is a constant with a comment that says why. Source half: no static import of a parser package in `src/`. Runtime half: `tests/editor/codeLanguagesLazy.test.ts` counts imports of `@codemirror/language-data` and sees none for documents with no fence word, `~~~` with a blank word, or an unlabeled fence, and exactly one for two views holding three languages. In the real window the language list was not requested for a document with no fenced block and was requested after a document with one was opened. 9 deliberate breaks of the rule (ignore modulepreload, skip static imports, allow the list, drop `lang-css` from the allowance, widen the allowance, Windows paths, pnpm store paths, `explicit` flag, skip scanning) were all caught by `tests/scripts/checkArch.test.ts` (92 tests); 4 breaks of the editor code (no extension lookup, no `codeLanguages`, an unknown token name in the style, a static import of the list) were caught by the two test files (P4-T4).

- ISC-6 (reopened 2026-11-08, by me): closed earlier at 11.2 ms (see the entry above), and not true at HEAD. `pnpm perf tab-switch` on the release app, five runs each: switch p95 52.7 ms (51.6 to 53.3) on e564f1c, 49.6 ms (48.4 to 51.0) on d5166ab (T4), 52.4 ms (52.1 to 55.1) on 708a90c (T1), 16.1 ms on c98e4d0 (end of Phase 3); against 50 ms. - ISC-6 (closed again 2026-11-08, owner approved the fix, commits 4a1383d, 2fe9bdf and 9d6f8e9): a table with more than 50 body rows is built as its header and first 50 rows plus one spacer row at the height of the rest; after idle time the real rows are swapped in during CodeMirror's write phase, with a measure request keyed by the table's own wrapper (`src/editor/livePreview/blocks/widgets.ts`). `pnpm perf tab-switch` on a fresh release build of 9d6f8e9, five runs: switch p95 18.7 ms (runs 20.3, 18.7, 18.7, 18.2, 20.2; repeatable) against 50 ms; median 15.6 ms, plain 16.3, with a change made elsewhere 14.5, synchronous part 3.6 ms. Before the fix: 52.7 ms. An earlier run of the same code before the per-table key (2fe9bdf) read 19.7 ms and the harness called it not repeatable (spread 11%); it is superseded. The cause was measured in the real window: the first screen of `tables-heavy-1mb.md` is one table of 2,907 cells that the view laid out whole (51 to 56 ms median for that switch against 15 ms with the block layer off; 17 to 18.5 ms after the change). Tests: `tests/editor/tableProgressive.test.ts` (10 tests); I broke the widget ten ways on purpose (every row at once, spacer never replaced, no re-measure, no cancel on destroy, click ignoring the row, one row short, spacer too short, rest in reverse order, swap outside the write step, one measure key shared by all tables) and each failed a test. A real mouse click on a row not yet drawn landed on the right source line. Limit unchanged. See decisions.md, "ISC-6 fixed".
- ISC-71 (reworded 2026-11-08 by the owner's decision, closed again): the claim was "Language parsers stay out of the entry bundle", which is not literally true because `@codemirror/lang-markdown` imports `lang-html`, `lang-css` and `lang-javascript` (and their Lezer parsers) itself. The owner chose to reword it to what the check enforces: "The entry bundle holds no language parser beyond the ones Markdown itself imports (HTML, CSS and JavaScript); every other language loads when a fence names it." Evidence is the entry above: the check reads the source map of every chunk the page loads first and fails on any other language package; its tests plant a parser in the entry and watch it fail.

- ISC-90 to ISC-94 (closed 2026-11-08, P5-T5): `pnpm test:web tests/editor/lists.test.ts` (the list editing block, 11 tests). Enter continues `-`, `*`, `1.` (as `2.`), a quoted list and a nested item (ISC-90); `- [x]` and `- [ ]` continue as `- [ ] ` (ISC-91); Enter on an empty item ends the list with a blank line after it, task box or not, and an empty nested item steps out one level (ISC-92); Tab nests an item under the one before it, children included, lined up with that item's text (`2.` under `1. ` gets three spaces) (ISC-93); Shift+Tab moves a nested item out one level, children included (ISC-94); the two undo each other; a first or top-level item stays put but the key is taken; read-only documents are left alone; the keys sit above CodeMirror's Markdown keymap in every follower. Real window (trusted keys): `- one` Enter, `two` Tab, Enter, `[x] done` Enter gave `  - [ ] `; Enter there stepped out to `- [ ] `; Enter again ended the list, and text typed then was a paragraph. 11 deliberate breaks each failed a test.

- ISC-179 (second by-hand check, 2026-11-09): the owner dragged again in the debug app on the external monitor (devicePixelRatio 1), undid with Ctrl+Z, dropped a file from Explorer and dragged again, and tried Tab and Shift+Tab: all worked. - Breaks on purpose in the T7 code: 24 in the Rust index (each point of the score, the letter-order prefilter, case, spaces, the query cap, root names, the cap and its notice, rebuild on every query, `.git`, order for an empty query, total, limit, UTF-16 offsets, the name start, the path back through the table, refresh): 20 failed a test at once, 1 did not apply until its text was fixed and then failed a test, and 3 survived: the word-start bonus after a slash, the name start, and a rebuild on every query. The first two were fixed with new tests and are caught on a second run; the third is equivalent (a second look at the same cache returns the same index), so it stays. 39 in the page (scorer points, the query cap, shield, greyed command, stale answer, wrapping, Tab, Escape, Enter, backdrop, row reset on a new search, footer, refresh, failure, page keys, toggle, focus, rows, shortcut mapping, the mock): 31 failed a test at once and 8 survived (leading gap, word-start bonus, exact-case bonus, the query cap, a stale answer replacing a newer one, the row reset on a new search, PageUp and PageDown, focus taken back after a file opened) and were fixed with new tests; all 8 are caught on a second run. The first stale-answer test depended on timers and passed with the guard removed on one run; it now holds the slow answer with a promise it releases itself. The `palette-lazy` arch rule fails on a planted static import and on three planted tree variants (P6-T7).

- ISC-255: Page (`tests/features/treeFocus.test.ts`, the tree and the panes drawn together): a click opens the file and `document.activeElement` stays the tree, for a new tab, for the tab already shown and for a second file; a double click puts the caret in the editor and a double click on a folder toggles it once (the second click of a pair, `detail >= 2`, is ignored); Space keeps focus on the tree and Enter moves the caret in, also when the file is already the shown tab; a link or Quick Open (`openPath`) still focuses the editor; a tab clicked in the strip does, the shown tab included; a second pane that becomes active during the hold does not take focus; the hold (`holdingEditorFocus`) is off after the work, also when it throws. `tests/features/openInPane.test.ts` still pins who opens in the active pane (its regex changed on purpose, since the tree now wraps the call). Opens are serialized, so a double click focuses the editor only after its first click has shown the file. Broken on purpose, each caught: the click does not hold focus (6 fail), the `detail >= 2` guard removed (1 fails), the `active` watcher ignores the hold (1 fails). Real debug window (`r3_1.cjs` 28/28, `r3_1b.cjs` 6/6, trusted CDP presses, a double click sent as presses with clickCount 1 then 2): after a click the tree has focus and the editor has no `cm-focused`; Space keeps it, Enter and a double click put the caret in the editor (`cm-focused`), a double click on a folder toggled it once and again once; Quick Open on a new file and on the file already shown put the caret in the editor; a tab click does. A real-window run found two faults the page tests could not: a click on the already shown tab left focus on the tree (fixed with `focusActiveEditor()` in the tab strip's select handler), and Vite serving two copies of `focus.ts` after a stash (a dev-server artifact, the cause of one false failure; fixed by touching the importers, not a code defect).

Real debug window (`r3_1.cjs`, `r3_1b.cjs`): after a rename the focus is on the tree, after Escape of a rename too, after a Delete confirmed in the dialog (`bb.md` gone from disk) too; with a rename that asks "Update links?", focus sat on the banner button while it was up and was back on the tree after both Update links and Don't update; the selected row's background was `oklch(0.3 0.016 262)` with the tree focused and `oklab(... / 0.6)` without (screenshot checked: a subtle fill, no ring). - ISC-260: Page: a 10 MB state with no parser finds its last match from the top; `countMatches` with a cap of 500 returns 500 and `capped` true; with a budget of 0 ms a rare word at the very end gives total 0 and `capped` true (the clock is read between pieces, not only after a match: without that a rare word scanned all 10 MB); a 1.3 MB document of several pieces is counted exactly, with matches on the lines right after each join, none missed and none twice. The bar says "Count stopped" when time ran out with nothing found, never "No results". Real window (`count-timing.mjs`, the debug build's WebView2, 10,850,028 characters, five runs each): a rare word 20.4 to 23.3 ms, a word that is absent 20.7 to 25.7 ms, the letter "e" 9.8 to 13.1 ms (stopped at the 10,000 cap). A first version with a 40 ms budget and 512 KB pieces measured 51 to 71 ms for the same words, which is why the budget is 20 ms and the pieces 128 KB. The count runs once per change of query or selection and after a 250 ms pause in typing, so on a file this large a rare word costs about 25 ms of main thread per Enter in the box; typing during an open bar is measured by `typing-find-open` at the end of Phase 7.

- ISC-327 (P9-T5, partly): `cargo test draft_images`: a paste into an untitled document lands in `drafts/<number>/assets/image-….png`, a second one in the same second gets `-2`, another untitled document has a folder of its own, a dropped picture is always copied into the draft folder (even one inside a workspace folder), and a missing drafts folder is refused; `tests/editor/imagePaste.test.ts` and `tests/features/dropImage.test.ts` check the link in the text and the mock's draft path. Real window (debug `mdmate.exe` over my own Vite, own MDMATE_DATA_DIR and WebView2 profile, generated fixtures, DevTools port, 150% display scale, 2026-11-13): Ctrl+N made Untitled-1; a screenshot paste and a two-file Explorer copy paste wrote three files under `data/drafts/1/assets/` (a `.png` from the screenshot, a `.jpg` and a `.png` from the file copy) and three links `assets/image-….`; all three drew (naturalWidth 240, 320, 160) through `http://asset.localhost/`. A second untitled tab (Untitled-2) got `drafts/2/`, its picture drew, and a tab switch away and back kept it drawn. After a hard kill and a relaunch the restored Untitled-2 drew its picture from `drafts/2/`. A real OLE drop (separate window, real mouse, drag result `Copy`) of a file outside every folder onto Untitled-8 copied it to `drafts/8/assets/image-….jpg`, inserted `![](assets/image-….jpg)` and drew it (naturalWidth 160).
- ISC-331 (P9-T5): `cargo test draft_images_first_save_as`: two used draft images and one unused; the target already has the first name, so it is renamed `-2`, its link in the written file is updated, the owner's picture is byte-equal afterwards, the unused image stays behind, the draft folder is gone. Also: a cancelled dialog keeps the draft, a failed write takes the copies back and keeps the draft, a target inside the drafts folder is refused. `tests/documents/draftImages.test.ts` checks the page: the same rename applied to the document's text as a change history does not record, the document not edited-looking afterwards. The link rule (which `assets/<name>` counts, which does not) is one set of 15 cases in `tests/data/draft-links.json` that both `cargo test draft_images_link_rule` and `tests/documents/draftLinks.test.ts` run, so the two sides cannot drift.
- ISC-332 (P9-T5, partly): `cargo test draft_images`: `remove` takes one folder and only it, `sweep` takes only all-digit direct children that no tab keeps and never enters a link or junction (a real junction test), a `drafts` folder that is itself a link is not used or swept; `tests/documents/draftImages.test.ts`: the folder stays while the close is undecided and when the question is cancelled, goes with Discard, at once for an unedited tab, and through Close Others (the kept tab's folder stays) and Close All; `tests/documents/session.test.ts` (`draft folders`): a restored tab finds its folder again and a new image lands in it, an unlisted draft is swept after a clean restore, nothing is swept after a restore with a notice or on a first start, and the text of a tab that could not come back is recovered with the images it links to beside the recovered file (`cargo test draft_images_recovered`). Real window (debug `mdmate.exe` over my own Vite, own MDMATE_DATA_DIR and WebView2 profile, generated fixtures, DevTools port, 150% display scale, 2026-11-13): Ctrl+W on a dirty untitled tab (text and a pasted picture, `drafts/7/`) showed the bar "Save the changes to Untitled-7 before closing?" with Save, Don't save and Cancel; Cancel kept the tab and `drafts/7/`; Ctrl+W again then Don't save closed the tab and `drafts/` was empty. Save As removed `drafts/1` (see ISC-331). An unowned `drafts/9` made before a relaunch was gone after the restore (swept); one made after the restore was still there after a plain close (only a restore sweeps it). Quit: the window closed with `CloseMainWindow` (a graceful close, not a kill) with `drafts/8/` holding a picture; the process exited, `drafts/8/` was still there, and the relaunch restored Untitled-8 with its picture drawn from `drafts/8/` (naturalWidth 240). A hard kill and relaunch did the same for `drafts/2/`.

  Breaks that failed a test, each put back afterwards: the editor not asking the app first (ISC-402, in the standalone state and in both tab states); resolving once instead of until nothing collides, and dropping only one side of a collision (ISC-403); the recorder not stopping the key (ISC-406); a recorded default staying in the file (ISC-375); `null` read as unusable (ISC-404, ISC-374).
  Real window (debug exe rebuilt with the new Rust, my own Vite, own data folder and WebView2 profile, real key events over the debugging port, fixtures made for the run): the Keyboard group listed 41 rows with the registry's shortcuts. Clicking New File and pressing Ctrl+Shift+K wrote `{ "file.new": "Mod+Shift+K" }`, Ctrl+N then opened no tab and Ctrl+Shift+K opened one (ISC-373). Ctrl+Shift+B on Bold showed "Ctrl+Shift+B is used by Toggle Sidebar. Replace Cancel" with the file unchanged, and Replace wrote `view.toggleSidebar: null` and `format.bold: "Mod+Shift+B"` (ISC-374). A hand edit of the file changed the row at once. Shift alone was ignored, a plain K showed "Keybinding "K" needs Mod or Alt, or it would swallow typing", Escape left the old shortcut and the file alone (ISC-406). Reset all wrote `{}` and left the button off (ISC-408). The palette command opened a `keybindings.json` tab (ISC-409). With the editor focused, Ctrl+Shift+K (CodeMirror's delete line) took a line off a three-line draft; after Toggle Word Wrap was bound to it, the same key left the lines and wrote `{ "editor": { "wordWrap": false } }` (ISC-402). With unsaved edits in the `keybindings.json` tab, a rebind showed "keybindings.json has changes you have not saved. Save them before changing New File?" with the file unchanged, and Save wrote the tab and the change (ISC-405). Screenshots of the conflict and the question were read.
  Found by the real run and fixed: the first version added the app-first handler only to the standalone editor state, and the app opens tabs with the follower state, so a rebinding onto a CodeMirror key still lost in the window while the jsdom test passed. The handler is now in both, and ISC-402's test runs against the standalone state and both follower states.
  Not checked: the notice for invalid entries (ISC-376) in the window (jsdom only); Cancel and Don't save on the question in the window; the Keyboard group in Light by eye; the shortcut hints in the tab strip and the tree menu after a rebind (ISC-411 was narrowed to the Keyboard row, the palette and the menu, which the test reads; the others read the same function); Mac Cmd and a non-US layout (Ctrl+Alt is AltGr on many Windows layouts, and the recorder accepts it); keys the system or WebView2 takes before the page sees them (F5, F11, Alt+F4); a rebinding onto a key CodeMirror uses for typing, which now wins by design; the order of the rows (A to Z by title, no grouping or filter).

  Breaks that failed a test, each put back afterwards: search ignoring the shortcut text; a word with a plus read as a plain substring (so `ctrl+s` finds Close All); an empty group keeping its heading; groups in A to Z order; `registerCommand` not checking the group; Escape not clearing the box; choosing a category keeping the old query.
  Real window (debug exe, own Vite, data folder and WebView2 profile, real key and text events over the debugging port): six headings in the agreed order over 41 rows, File and Tabs & Navigation rows A to Z, the box on the title row (same row, right edge inside the panel). Typing `split` left only Tabs & Navigation with Split Down and Split Right; `ctrl+s` left Save and Save As; `toggle` left View then App; `formatting` left the 7 formatting rows; `zzzz` showed "No shortcut matches that."; Escape brought back all 41 rows and 6 headings. Search settings with `save` showed no shortcut button and no shortcut box. Screenshots of the top of the page, `ctrl+s` and the empty result were looked at.

- settings.json and keybindings.json as JSON with comments (2026-11-14, after the Keyboard groups): ISC-420 to ISC-430 closed. `tests/lib/fileTemplate.test.ts` (21) pins the template layout, every setting key and every command id, the template reading as no entries, comments surviving edits, and the round trip to the exact template text: each key set then reset, all keys in set order and reverse order, lines and whole groups uncommented by hand then reset from the screen, and 150 seeded random mixes of untouched, set and uncommented keys reset in random order. `tests/features/commentedFiles.test.ts` (21) runs both open commands on a missing, an empty and a `{}` file (template written), a file with content (untouched) and a tab with unsaved edits (untouched); a change keeping the user's own comments; Reset all; a comments-only file counting as no entries (Reset all off); comments and trailing commas with no banner; a syntax error naming line 4 and line 3; an uncommented line taking effect for a setting and a shortcut; the first change starting from the template; a change that changes nothing writing no file. `tests/editor/jsonComments.test.ts` (7) covers the comment colouring. `pnpm check`, `pnpm test:web` (2328 passed), `pnpm test:rust` (576 passed) and `pnpm check:rust` pass.

Edits that add or remove a key are now by line; `jsonc-parser` still changes values in place and fills an empty object.

Real window (own rig, port 9338, a draft with a pasted PNG): with loud colors the outline and the square both read rgb(0, 255, 255) (the `--handle-active` set) at rest, under the pointer and while dragged; Dark, Light and Sepia read their own `--handle-active` the same way; screenshots read for the loud theme and Sepia. Not checked: that the drag still resizes the picture in the real window after this change (only colors changed; the drag was not measured).

## Remaining Work

- [x] A block layer edge case found while fixing ISC-334: a table that a selection touches is not drawn, so nothing remembers how far it reached. An edit that makes it swallow the lines after it (a picture two lines below becomes a row) leaves that picture drawn, and an edit that makes it let them go leaves a picture undrawn until the next change near it. Reached only by a random-edit property test (seed 11, picture-and-table document); not seen in use. Fixing it needs the field to keep each hidden block's extent. Not part of Phase 9. Fixed in Phase 9 (P9-T5 follow-up): see the Decisions row of 2026-11-13.
